PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT BOARD AGENDA
The regular meeting of the PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT BOARD will be held at 6:00 p.m. on TUESDAY, FEBRUARY 24, 2026 in the
Common Council Chambers on the third floor of City Hall, 108 E Green Street. The meeting will also be streamed on the
City of Ithaca YouTube Channel at https://www.youtube.com/channel/UC7RtJN1P_RFaFW2IVCnTrDg.
Instructions for commenting to the Planning Board
Scheduled Public Hearings (Refer to the agenda for projects that have scheduled public hearings.)
There are two options to participate in a Public Hearing:
1. Submit comments by email no later than 12 p.m. on the day of the meeting (contacts below). Each comment is
limited to three minutes. Indicate in your email that the comment is for a public hearing.
2. Attend the meeting via Zoom and speak directly to the Board. Comments are limited to three minutes.
General Public Comments
Public comments are heard at the beginning of the meeting via Zoom. Written comments should be sent to the contact(s)
listed below. All comments received will be forwarded to the Planning Board for their consideration. Written comments
received in advance of the meeting give the Board/Committee time to consider them fully. If you want your comment read
aloud, please state so in your email and limit the comment to three minutes.
Please use the following contacts to submit comments or request access to the Zoom meeting: Planning Board Staff at
Pbstaff@cityofithaca.org or call 607-274-6550.
Start Times: Start times are approximate only — APPLICANTS are responsible for being available at whatever time(s) their agenda item(s) is actually discussed.

Project Description: The Planning Board granted preliminary and final approval of this project on

now proposes changes to the site plan due to newly adopted FEMA floodplain and regulatory floodway

Site Plan Review comments from the City of Ithaca Engineering Office
Feb 17, 2026
1) Project: The Citizen: 602 W Buffalo St.
a. Remove storm doghouse manhole and connect to existing catchbasin west of
driveway entrance (RIM 386.6). This is advantageous because it reduces
stormwater maintenance in the private driveway entrance and is a preferred
connection.
b. Of note: NYS DOT is planning a major asphalt paving project on both sides of this
project. It is highly encouraged to do your sub-surface utility connections prior
to the new pavement installation—anticipated this spring.
c. Basic SWPPP was reviewed and accepted for this project in 2023. Provide
updated SWPPP to incorporate recent changes or verify that any changes do not
impact the accepted SWPPP.
2) Project: The Hive : 132 Cherry Street
a. Upload separate Site Plan drawings (i.e. C-005)—current scan is blurry and
unreadable.
b. Project will disturb >1ac and will require a Full SWPPP. Submit
to stormwater@cityofithaca.org for review.
c. Existing stormpipe under new proposed driveway will need to be replaced due to
proposed traffic load with HDPE pipe.
d. Note: It appears that this current proposal would remove the street parking
proposal.
3) Project: Finger Lakes Reuse Site Improvements: 214 Elmira Road
a. Note: Recommended 1’ sump in all private catchbasins.
4) Project: 309 College Avenue Apartments: 309 College Avenue
a. NYSEG Vault to align with other underground vaults in the treelawn areas. Vault
lid to be <2% cross-slope.
b. Show proposed sidewalk topo and running slopes at transitions. Running
sidewalk slope should not be steeper than existing curbline.
c. Show a proposed construction staging plan and work zone traffic control plan.
d. For the Street Permit, a Letter of Special Conditions will likely be needed.
e. Note: Use existing stormwater structures.

5. Subdivision Review
A. 614-722 S Meadow Street - Creekside Plaza Major Subdivision/Parcel
Reconfiguration by ROF TA Ithaca LLC. Consideration of Final Subdivision Approval.
The Applicant proposes to reconfigure of four tax parcels (102.-1-2.1, 102.-1-2.3,
102.-1-2.6 and 102.-1-2.4), that together measure approximately 20.5 acres with
more than 900 feet of frontage on S. Meadow Street, and contain commercial
buildings, parking, two curb cuts/ access points, and other associated site
improvements. The applicant proposes to create six new parcels: Parcel 1 measuring
approximately 3.795 acres with approximately 200 feet of frontage on S. Meadow
Street and containing existing commercial buildings and parking; Parcel 2 measuring
7.335 acres containing the one access point off of S. Meadow St and commercial
buildings and parking; Parcel 3 measuring 7.338 acres containing one access point
off of S. Meadow and containing an existing commercial building and parking area;
and Parcel 4 measuring .343 acres with approximately 100 feet of frontage on S.
Meadow Street; Parcel 5 measuring .603 acres with approximately 192 feet of
frontage on S. Meadow Street and containing an existing commercial building
(Elmira Savings Bank) and parking area ; Parcel 6 measuring 1.058 acres with 340.7
feet of frontage on S. Meadow Street and containing an existing commercial building
(Verizon Wireless) and parking. This project is in the SW-2 zoning district and will
require area variances. This is an Unlisted Action under the City of Ithaca
Environmental Quality Review Ordinance and the State Environmental Quality
Review Act and is subject to environmental review.
Applicants Attending: Phebe Perkin
The applicant confirmed the proposal to reconfigure multiple tax parcels resulting in a major
subdivision review and that no physical changes were proposed.
Rollman discussed with the applicant and the board a condition to install bicycle parking per
site plan requirements.
Adopted Declaration Final Subdivision Approval
Motion by Godden, seconded by Rollman.
6. Site Plan Review
A. The Citizen – 602 W Buffalo Street by Visum Development Group, LLC. Potential
Amended Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance. The applicant
proposes to construct a new 4-story apartment building approximately 75,250 SF
gross floor area. The building will contain 59 residential units, a community room,
support spaces, bike storage, 2,307 sq ft of retail, and a ground floor parking area
with 27 parking spaces. The project is located in the WEDZ-1a zoning district and is
expected to not require any variances. This is a Type 1 Action under the City of

Ithaca Environmental Quality Review Ordinance §176-4 B.(1)(k), (l), and (n), and the
State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) §617.4 b. (11) and is subject to

Applicants Attending: Julia Bucher, Bear Smith, Todd Fox
The applicants gave a brief presentation on a redesigned housing development proposed for

Board members asked clarifying questions regarding the façade of the building, the design of
the entrances to the building and parking garage. Board members also requested a more robust
landscape plan including the landscape beyond the property line.
B. 132 Cherry Street – The Hive by 132 Cherry Street Clinton LLC. Project Update –
Modified Site Plan Review. The Planning Board granted preliminary and final approval
of this project on September 27, 2022 and granted an extension for site plan approval
on September 26, 2024. The applicant now proposes changes to the site plan due to
newly adopted FEMA floodplain and regulatory floodway maps. The changes include
but are not limited to moving the location of both buildings, changing outdoor
amenities, and changing the unit count and square footage of the project. The project
is located in the Cherry Street District Zoning District and will require variance(s). This
has been determined to be a Type 1 Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental
Quality Review Ordinance §176-4 B(1) (h)[2], (k), and (n) and the State Environmental
Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) §617.4 (b)(11) for which the Planning Board, acting as
Lead Agency, issued a Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance on August
23, 2022.
Applicants Attending: Julia Bucher, Craig Jensen, Mariam Yaqub
The applicants presented several proposed modifications to the previously approved site plan.
These changes included adjustments to the placement, size and site plan of two residential
buildings and associated site improvements.
Board members were supportive of the project and the proposed changes. Board members
requested more information about the landscaping plan, loading and delivery parking spaces
for the building as well as other operational plans.

Dear Planning & Development Board members & staff,
Two quick comments/questions:
1. Bicycle racks & Creekside Plaza Major Subdivision – is this an opportunity to address
missing bike racks per current design guidelines? Was informed by a retail manager in
another SW retail center that bike racks are controlled by the property owners, not the
individual store lessees.
As a resident using a bicycle for most of my errands throughout the year, I have not been able to find
bike racks near stores in most of this shopping plaza.
i. Only the new Dicks sporting goods store has one that meets the design
guidelines.
ii. Barnes & Noble has a very unsatisfactory non-commercial, old fashioned free
standing one for setting the front wheel in that is designed for kid’s bikes, such
that the frame of a large wheel 10+ speed can only be locked if leaned against the
end of the rack. Needs to be replaced with some U ones sufficiently spaced out
from the wall that a bike frame can be fastened to the U posts, not just a wheel
(i.e., not too close to the building wall as so many of them are).
iii. There are no bike racks serving the businesses in the south parcel where Empire
Vision, Sherwin Williams, some eateries, EMS, and Verizon Wireless are located.
Nothing to lock to and must lean the bike on a wall.
2. Am in strong agreement with need for The Citizen to wrap the visual feel of a main entry all
the way around the corner from Buffalo St & across the back (parking) side of the building,
given that this really is a main entrance for residents and their visitors. What about signaling
that entrance with some elements on the Buffalo St columns where one turns off the
sidewalk to walk to the back entrance, and repeating the mural aesthetic along the west
entry side? Materiality and lighting along the west/parking-side of building should feel like a
main entry and welcoming.
Would really be useful to see a rendering of the backside of the building from the perspective of the
pedestrian access walk and its treatment as formal entrance. Otherwise, I really appreciate the
building design and struggle to keep the aesthetic and functionality of the retail area while
addressing the new flood plain issue.
Thanks for your careful consideration of all the elements of the site plans and changes that come
before you! As I watch the meetings, I have much respect and appreciation for the care you take in
sharing your concerns and suggestions with the project teams.
Sheryl
Sheryl N. Swink

ADOPTED RESOLUTION Major Subdivision Review
Final Approval 614-722 S Meadow St Tax Parcels #102.-1-
2.1, 102.-1-2.3, 102.-1-2.6 and 102.-1-2.4
December 16, 2025
WHEREAS: an application has been submitted for review and approval by the City of Ithaca Planning
and Development Board for a Major Subdivision at 719-725 S Meadow St by ROF TA Ithaca LLC,
owner, and
WHEREAS: the applicant proposes to reconfigure of four tax parcels (102.-1-2.1, 102.-1-2.3, 102.-1-2.6
and 102.-1-2.4), that together measure approximately 20.5 acres with more than 900 feet of frontage on S.
Meadow Street, and contain commercial buildings, parking, two curb cuts/ access points, and other
associated site improvements. The applicant proposes to create six new parcels: Parcel 1 measuring
approximately 3.795 acres with approximately 200 feet of frontage on S. Meadow Street and containing
existing commercial buildings and parking; Parcel 2 measuring 7.335 acres containing the one access
point off of S. Meadow St and commercial buildings and parking; Parcel 3 measuring 7.338 acres
containing one access point off of S. Meadow and containing an existing commercial building and
parking area; and Parcel 4 measuring .343 acres with approximately 100 feet of frontage on S. Meadow
Street; Parcel 5 measuring .603 acres with approximately 192 feet of frontage on S. Meadow Street and
containing an existing commercial building (Elmira Savings Bank) and parking area ; Parcel 6 measuring
1.058 acres with 340.7 feet of frontage on S. Meadow Street and containing an existing commercial
building (Verizon Wireless) and parking. This project is in the SW-2 zoning district and will require no
variances, and
WHEREAS: this is considered a Major Subdivision in accordance with the City of Ithaca Code, Chapter
290, Article 1, §290-1, Major Subdivision – Any subdivision of land resulting in the creation of two or
more additional buildable lots, and
WHEREAS: This has been determined to be an Unlisted Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental
Quality Review Ordinance and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) and is subject to
environmental review, and
WHEREAS: City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board, being that local agency which has primary
responsibility for approving and funding or carrying out the action, did, on November 24, 2025, declare
itself Lead Agency in Environmental Review for the proposed project, and
WHEREAS: legal notice was published and property posted, and adjacent property owners notified in
accordance with Chapters 290-9 C. (1), (2), & (3) of the City of Ithaca Code, and
WHEREAS: the Planning and Development Board held the required Public Hearing on November 24,
2025, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board, acting as Lead Agency in Environmental Review, did on November 24,
2025 review and accept as adequate: a Short Environmental Assessment Form (SEAF), Part 1, submitted
by the applicant, and Part 2, prepared by Planning staff; a drawing titled Subdivision Map, of the Lands of
G&I IX Empire Tops Plaza Ithaca LLC, Located on South Meadow Street – State Route 13, City of
Ithaca, Tompkins County, New York, dated 02/25/21 and prepared by TG Miller P.C. and other
application materials, and
WHEREAS: the Tompkins County Planning Department, and other interested parties have been given the
opportunity to comment on the proposed project and any received comments have been considered, and

WHEREAS: the Lead Agency made a Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance on November
24, 2025, and
WHEREAS: this Board, acting as Lead Agency in Environmental Review, has on November 24, 2025,
reviewed and accepted as adequate: a Full Environmental Assessment Form (FEAF), Part 1, submitted by
the applicant, and Parts 2 prepared by Planning staff; an undated drawing titled: “ALTA/NSPS Land Title
614, 702 and 710-34 S. Meadow Street, Ithaca, NY 14850” prepared Blew & Associates, P.A. and other
application materials, and
WHEREAS: the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board did on November 24, 2025 grant
preliminary subdivision approval to the project subject to the following conditions:
Before certification/signature of the Final Subdivision plat:
i. Submission to Planning staff of an assessment of existing bicycle racks, along with a proposal to
bring the properties into compliance with, or to exceed, the City’s bicycle parking standards,
including the installation of the proposed bicycle parking.
ii. Submission of three paper copies of the final approved plat, all having a raised seal and signature
of a registered licensed surveyor, now, therefore, be it
RESOLVED: that the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board does herby grant final subdivision
approval to the project subject to the above conditions.
Moved by: Godden
Seconded by: Rollman
In favor: Godden. Rollman. Petrina, Pfeffer, Tully
Against: None
Abstain: None
Absent: Sutcliffe
Vacancies: One

A. The Citizen – 602 W Buffalo Street by Visum Development Group, LLC.
Presentation. The applicant proposes to construct a new 4-story apartment building
approximately 75,250 SF gross floor area. The building will contain 59 residential
units, a community room, support spaces, bike storage, 2,307 sq ft of retail, and a
ground floor parking area with 27 parking spaces. The project is located in the
WEDZ-1a zoning district and is expected to not require any variances. This is a Type 1
Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental Quality Review Ordinance §176-4
B.(1)(k), (l), and (n), and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”)
§617.4 b. (11) and is subject to environmental review.
Applicants Attending: Julia Bucher, Bear Smith, Todd Fox
The applicants presented changes made to the proposed residential building since the previous
presentation. Changes included an enlargement of the proposed mural, the relocation of
entrances, and the location of the proposed architectural fins for the building. The applicants
also highlighted elements of the proposed landscaping plan.
Board members discussed with the applicant the affordable housing elements of the proposed
development, the design of the parking garage exterior and voiced support for the relocation of
the entrances and enlargement of the mural.
B. 132 Cherry Street – The Hive by 132 Cherry Street Clinton LLC. Project Update –
Modified Site Plan Review. The Planning Board granted preliminary and final approval
of this project on September 27, 2022 and granted an extension for site plan approval
on September 26, 2024. The applicant now proposes changes to the site plan due to
newly adopted FEMA floodplain and regulatory floodway maps. The changes include
but are not limited to moving the location of both buildings, changing outdoor
amenities, and changing the unit count and square footage of the project. The project
is located in the Cherry Street District Zoning District and will require variance(s). This
has been determined to be a Type 1 Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental
Quality Review Ordinance §176-4 B(1) (h)[2], (k), and (n) and the State Environmental
Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) §617.4 (b)(11) for which the Planning Board, acting as
Lead Agency, issued a Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance on August
23, 2022.
Applicants Attending: Julia Bucher, Craig Jensen, Mariam Yaqub
The applicants presented several proposed modifications to the previously approved site plan.
These changes included adjustments to the placement, size and site plan of two residential
buildings and associated site improvements.
Board members discussed with the applicant the appropriate layout for street parking in
relation to the proposed landscaping plan as well as the proposal for landscaping improvements
for both public and private use.

WHEREAS: 6 NYCRR Part 617 of the State Environmental Quality Review Law and Chapter 176.6 of
the City Code, Environmental Quality Review, require that a lead agency be established for conducting
environmental review of projects in accordance with local and state environmental law, and
WHEREAS: State Law specifies that for actions governed by local environmental review, the lead
agency shall be that local agency which has primary responsibility for approving and funding or carrying
out the action, and
WHEREAS: the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board has one pending application for site
plan approval for constructing a new 8-story apartment building by Nick Roberston, and
WHEREAS: the applicant proposes to demolish an existing 1-story fire station and construct a new 8-
story 55,000 SF mixed-use apartment building with 77 units and ground story 1500 SF retail space. The
project is located in the MU2 and will not require any variances. It is subject to Collegetown Design
Guidelines, and
WHEREAS: this has been determined to be a Type 1 Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental
Quality Review Ordinance §176-4 B(1)(k) & (l), and the State Environmental Quality Review Act
(“SEQRA”) §617.4 (11) and is subject to environmental review, and
WHEREAS: the NYS Department of Environmental Conservation, and the Tompkins County
Department of Health, both potentially involved agencies in this action, have consented to the Planning
Board acting as Lead Agency for this project, now, therefore be it
RESOLVED: that the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board does, by way of this resolution,
declare itself Lead Agency in Environmental Review for the proposed project.

out the action, and

PROPOSED RESOLUTION City of Ithaca Planning & Development Board
Final & Preliminary Approval The Citizen
602 W. Buffalo St
Site Plan Approval
February 24, 2026
WHEREAS: the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board has one pending application for site plan
approval for constructing a 4-story apartment building by Visum Development Group, LLC, and
WHEREAS: proposes to construct a new 4-story apartment building approximately 75,250 SF gross
floor area. The building will contain 59 residential units, a community room, support spaces, bike
storage, 2,307 sq ft of retail, and a ground floor parking area with 27 parking spaces. After filing the
Negative Declaration, the applicant was required to alter the design to address the newly issued FEMA
flood maps as the building is now located in the 100-year floodplain, Zone AE flood hazard area.
Changes include raising the residential portion of the building by 4’ to an elevation of 392’ which is 2’
above Base Flood Elevation (BFE), dry flood proofing the retail space, wet flood proofing the under
building parking, building access under the building, and the trash and recycling room, and changes to
masonry and window heights along the streetscapes, and
WHEREAS: this is a Type 1 Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental Quality Review Ordinance
§176-4 B.(1)(k), (l), and (n), and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) §617.4 b. (11)
and is subject to environmental review, and
WHEREAS: the NYS Department of Environmental Conservation, the Ithaca Area Economic
Development, and the Tompkins County Department of Health, all potentially involved agencies in this
action, have consented to the Planning Board acting as Lead Agency for this project, and
WHEREAS: the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board, being the local agency, which has primary
responsibility for approving and funding or carrying out the action, did on September 27, 2022 declare itself
Lead Agency in Environmental Review for the project, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board, acting as Lead Agency in Environmental Review, did on January 31,
2022, review and accept as adequate: a Full Environmental Assessment Form (FEAF), Part 1, submitted
by the applicant, and Parts 2 and 3 prepared by Planning staff; Boundary & Topographic Map 602 W
Buffalo Street City of Ithaca, Tompkins County, New York State prepared by T.G. Miller, P.C. dated
12/22/21; Demolition Plan C-1.0, Site Plan C-2.0, Marked-up Site Plan C-2.0, Grading, Drainage &
Erosion Control Plan C-3.0, Utility Plan C-4.0, Landscape Plan C-5.0, Detail Sheet C-6.0-6.3 (4), and
Truck Turn Plan C-7.0 all prepared by Holt Architects and Marathon Engineering and dated 01/16/23;
Rendered Perspectives (2) and Shadow Studies (3) submitted 12/13/22 and prepared by Holt Architects;
Exterior Elevations A201 & A202 prepared by Holt Architects and dated 8/14/22; ;and other application
materials, and
WHEREAS: interested parties have been given the opportunity to comment on the proposed project, and
any received comments have been considered, and
WHEREAS: legal notice was published and property posted in accordance with Chapters 276-6 B. (4)
and 176-12 A. (2) (c) of the City of Ithaca Code, and
WHEREAS: the Planning and Development Board held the required public hearing beginning on
September 27, 2022, and

WHEREAS: the Planning Board did, on January 31, 2023 determine, as elaborated in the FEAF Part 3,
that the proposed project will result in no significant adverse impacts on the environment and issued a
Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance, and
WHEREAS: in accordance with §176-7 E. of CEQR and §617.7(e) of SEQRA, the City of Ithaca
Planning and Development Board acting as Lead Agency has determined that (1) new information has
been discovered and (2) a change in circumstances related to the project has arisen that was not
previously considered, and the Lead Agency has determined that no significant adverse impact will occur,
and
WHEREAS: the Board, acting as Lead Agency in environmental review, has on December 16, 2025
reviewed and accepted as adequate the new information consisting of: a revised Full Environmental
Assessment Form (FEAF), Part1, submitted by the applicant and Parts 2 and 3, prepared by Planning
staff; the following revised drawings: Building Rendering AP001-AP003, First Floor Accessibility
Option A- Schematic Plan AP100.A, First Floor Accessibility Option B- Schematic Plan AP100.B, First
Floor Plan AP101, Second Floor Plan AP102, Exterior Elevations – With Context AP201.1, Exterior

and other supporting materials, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board did, on December 16, 2025 determine, as elaborated in the FEAF Part
3, that the proposed project will result in no significant adverse impacts on the environment and issued an
Amended Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance, and
WHEREAS: this Board did on February 24, 2026 review and accept as adequate the following new and
revised drawings: New Construction ‘The Citizen Apartments” (12 pp.) C-1. –C-8.1 all dated 02/18/26

February 18th, 2026
Planning & Development Board
City of Ithaca
108 E Green St.
Ithaca, NY 14850
Re: PRC Meeting (2/13) Follow-up Items | The Citizen, 602 W Buffalo Street
HOLT Project No. 19030
Dear Planning Board and Staff,
This letter is to aid in tracking and responding to items requested by the Board at the last
Planning Review Committee (PRC) meeting. It is our intent to also review and discuss these
items during the upcoming Site Plan Review (SPR) Meeting.
Garage Screening
During the Project Review Committee (PRC) meeting, the design team was asked to identify
local multifamily projects featuring parking that is not fully enclosed (situated either under or
behind the buildings). In response, we have included Sheet AP901 in the drawing package.
This design approach is not only well-precedented within the City of Ithaca but represents the
standard methodology for this type of parking configuration.
Subsequent discussions with the Planning Board focused on balancing effective parking
screening with the need for visual security. To address the Board’s feedback, we have
extended the kneewall located behind the bike rack up to the roof deck. While this adjustment
largely screens the parked vehicles, the overall facade continues to allow for "eyes on the
street" and visual permeability through the double driveway entry and the west elevation.
Regarding the western property boundary, our proposal maintains the installation of a 6’–8’
fence along the property line. In our experience, when larger multifamily developments abut
single-family or smaller-scale properties, residents typically request privacy fencing. Detail A2
on Page AP200 illustrates how the specified fencing offers adaptable levels of privacy: from
the single-family backyards, the view into the parking is largely obstructed when viewed at a
90-degree angle; however, looking southeast from that same vantage point, there is a nearly
unobstructed view back toward the street and sidewalk.
Landscaping & Site Amenities
During the last meeting we heard that landscaping/ “urbanscaping” along the street was a
higher priority than seating along Meadow Street which we agree with. Therefore, we have
removed the seating and enlarged the planting area. This will also have the added benefit of
providing a better visual transition between grade and the higher windowsills due to the flood
plan.

As for the tree beds we heard concerns regarding the poured in place rubber and will instead
provide cast iron tree grates. Additionally, we heard that there was a desire to replace the brick
pavers. Upon inspection, the existing pavers are in good shape with some growth cropping up
between the pavers. As the existing tree beds are to be expanded the project will utilize the
bricks removed to replace broken or damaged bricks and clean up the undesirable growth.
This approach provides the best aesthetic in a sustainable manner.
Photometrics & Site Lighting
In the submission we have provided a photometric plan of the site, garage, and recessed
entries lighting. The targets we set to achieve are as follows, an average of 20 footcandles in
the parking garage for safety and security, an average of 10 footcandles at the entries, less an
average of 5 footcandles beyond the building footprint and 0 at the property line. For the
entries along the street, we did permit the lighting to spill off the property line to provide
additional lighting for the public sidewalk for community safety. These lights do not reach the
street.
While we believe this approach satisfies the goals and requirements for the project, we are
open to reducing the lighting as the board sees fit as a condition of approval. Our goal is to
provide a collaborative and balanced solution.
We hope these items aid the conversations along and we look forward to collaborating further
during our next meeting.
Sincerely,
HOLT Architects, P.C.
Steven W. Hugo, AIA
Principal

(CAN BE SLOEPXECDA OVRA TVIEORNT WICAALLL) B (40166 m"m) A DEPTH OF STONE BELOW CHAMBER, D NOTES: 12" (300 mm) MIN ESNCD- 3C1A0P SUBG(SREAED NEO STOEIL 3S) (150 m6m") MIN 34" (864 mm) 12" (300 mm) MIN 1.CHAMBERS SHALL MEET THE REQUIREMENTS OF ASTM F2922 (POLETHYLENE) OR ASTM F2418 (POLYPROPYLENE), "STANDARD SPECIFICATION FOR CORRUGATED WALL STORMWATER COLLECTION CHAMBERS".
2.SC-310 CHAMBERS SHALL BE DESIGNED IN ACCORDANCE WITH ASTM F2787 "STANDARD PRACTICE FOR STRUCTURAL DESIGN OF THERMOPLASTIC CORRUGATED WALL STORMWATER COLLECTION
CHAMBERS".
3.THE SITE DESIGN ENGINEER IS RESPONSIBLE FOR ASSESSING THE BEARING RESISTANCE (ALLOWABLE BEARING CAPACITY) OF THE SUBGRADE SOILS AND THE DEPTH OF FOUNDATION STONE WITH
CONSIDERATION FOR THE RANGE OF EXPECTED SOIL MOISTURE CONDITIONS.
4.PERIMETER STONE MUST BE EXTENDED HORIZONTALLY TO THE EXCAVATION WALL FOR BOTH VERTICAL AND SLOPED EXCAVATION WALLS. 5.REQUIREMENTS FOR HANDLING AND INSTALLATION:
(cid:120) TO MAINTAIN THE WIDTH OF CHAMBERS DURING SHIPPING AND HANDLING, CHAMBERS SHALL HAVE INTEGRAL, INTERLOCKING STACKING LUGS.
(cid:120) TO ENSURE A SECURE JOINT DURING INSTALLATION AND BACKFILL, THE HEIGHT OF THE CHAMBER JOINT SHALL NOT BE LESS THAN 2”.
(cid:120) TO ENSURE THE INTEGRITY OF THE ARCH SHAPE DURING INSTALLATION, a) THE ARCH STIFFNESS CONSTANT AS DEFINED IN SECTION 6.2.8 OF ASTM F2922 SHALL BE GREATER THAN OR EQUAL TO 400
LBS/FT/%. AND b) TO RESIST CHAMBER DEFORMATION DURING INSTALLATION AT ELEVATED TEMPERATURES (ABOVE 73° F / 23° C), CHAMBERS SHALL BE PRODUCED FROM REFLECTIVE GOLD OR YELLOW COLORS.

PROPOSED RESOLUTION City of Ithaca Planning & Development Board
S/CEQR – Amended Negative Declaration The Hive
132 Cherry St.
February 24, 2026
WHEREAS: on August 23, 2022, the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board determined that the
proposed mixed-use project “The Hive” site plan at 132 Cheery St would result in no significant impact on the
environment and that a Negative Declaration for purposes of Article 8 of the Environmental Conservation Law be
filed in accordance with the provisions of Part 617 of the State Environmental Quality Review Act, and
WHEREAS: the proposed project consisted of demolishing the existing building and constructing a primarily
residential mixed-use development. The applicant proposed two 5-story buildings, with a combined total gross
floor area of 174,622 SF. The buildings contained 143 residential units on four floors, three commercial spaces
totaling approximately 4,350 SF, 43 +/‐ parking spaces on the ground floor, and indoor amenities including a
fitness room, multifunctional studio, community kitchen, rooftop terrace and lounge, dog wash and secured
package room. Outdoor amenities included a picnic area, a waterfront courtyard with a pool, a plaza along
Cherry Street, streetscape improvements and landscaping. The project is located in in the Cherry Street District
Zoning District and will require a front yard variance, and
WHEREAS: this was a Type 1 Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental Quality Review Ordinance §176-4
B.(1)(k), (l), and (n), and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) §617.4 b. (11) and is subject
to environmental review, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board did, on September 27, 2022, grant the project Preliminary and Final Site Plan
Approval, and
WHEREAS: the project was not built and the site plan approval was extended on September 26, 2024, and
WHEREAS: the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board, being the local agency which has primary
responsibility for approving and funding or carrying out the action, did on April 26, 2022, declare itself Lead
Agency for the environmental review of the project, and
WHEREAS: the applicant now seeks modified site plan approval for the project with changes. The applicant now
proposes changes to the site plan due to newly adopted FEMA floodplain and regulatory floodway
maps. The changes include but are not limited to moving the location of both buildings, changing
outdoor amenities, and changing the unit count and square footage of the project. The project is located
in the Cherry Street District Zoning District and will require variance(s), and
WHEREAS: in accordance with §176-7 E. of CEQR and §617.7(e) of SEQRA, the City of Ithaca Planning and
Development Board acting as Lead Agency has determined that (1) new information has been discovered and (2)
a change in circumstances related to the project has arisen that was not previously considered, and the Lead
Agency has determined that no significant adverse impact will occur, and
WHEREAS: the Board, acting as Lead Agency in environmental review, has on February 24, 2026 reviewed and
accepted as adequate the new information consisting of: a revised Full Environmental Assessment Form (FEAF),
Part1, submitted by the applicant and Parts 2 and 3, prepared by Planning staff; the following revised drawings:
Boring Location S1 dated 02/6/2020 and prepared by Elwyn & Palmer Consulting Engineers, PLLC; Project
Vicinity Map prepared by Fagan Engineers; Construction Packet “The Hive” C-001-C13 (pp. 12) submitted
February 18, 2026 and prepared by CJS Architects and Fagan Engineers: Landscape Plan C-007, Tree Details C-
011, Ground Floor Key Plan A-110 (1) all submitted February 18, 2026 and prepared by CJS Architects; South

Building Elevations A-201 (1) & A-202 (1), North Building Elevations A-203 (1) & A-204 (1) all dated
1/21/2026 and prepared by CJS Architects; and other supporting materials, now therefore be it
RESOLVED: that the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board does hereby amend the Negative
Declaration issued on August 23, 2022, to include the above-mentioned information in the environmental record,
and be it further
RESOLVED: that based on all supporting documentation, the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board
does hereby determine that the proposed multiuse project The Hive located at 132 Cherry St will result in no
significant impact on the environment and that a Negative Declaration for purposes of Article 8 of the
Environmental Conservation Law be filed in accordance with the provisions of Part 617 of the State
Environmental Quality Review Act.

02/24/26 Amendment to the Negative Declaration issued on 8/23/22
City of Ithaca
FULL ENVIRONMENTAL ASSESSMENT FORM – Part III
Project Name: The Hive - 132 Cherry Street
Date Created: 05/03/22, Updated 5/10/22, 06/09/22, 06/20/22, 07/18/22, 08/04/22,
08/11/22, 08/16/22
Any text differing from the 8/23/22 FEAF is underlined
PROJECT DESCRIPTION
The applicant proposes to demolish the existing building and construct a primarily residential mixed-use
affordable housing development. The applicant proposes two 5-story buildings, with a combined total
gross floor area of 160,800 SF 174,622 SF. The buildings contain 148 143 residential units on four floors,
three commercial spaces totaling approximately 4,200 4,350 SF, 40 43 +/- parking spaces on the ground
floor, and indoor amenities including a fitness room, multifunctional studio, community room with
kitchen , rooftop terrace and lounge, dog wash, indoor bicycle storage, and secured package rooms.
Outdoor amenities include a picnic area, a waterfront courtyard with a pool, a plaza along Cherry Street,
streetscape improvements and landscaping. The project is located in in the Cherry Street District Zoning
District and will require a front yard several variances. The project site is in the Cherry Street Zoning
District (CSD) and is subject to Design Review.
This has been determined to be a Type 1 Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental Quality Review
Ordinance §176-4 B(1) (h)[2], (k), and (n) and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”)
§617.4 (b)(11) and is subject to environmental review for which Planning Board, acting as Lead Agency,
made a negative declaration of environmental significance on August 23, 2022.
After filing the Negative Declaration, the applicant was required to alter the design to address the newly
issued FEMA flood maps as the building is now located in the 100-year floodplain, Zone AE flood hazard
area. The design was also altered to meet New York State Homes and Community Renewal (HCR)
requirements as the applicants are pursuing HCR funding for affordable housing. Changes in design
include raising the residential lobbies by 42” so that they are 2’ above Base Flood Elevation (BFE),
installing ADU lifts in the lobbies, moving the buildings east towards Cherry Street, and reconfiguring the
building unit footprints and unit configuration.
In accordance with §176-7 E. of CEQR and §617.7(e) of SEQRA, the Lead Agency decided to amend this
negative determination in order to consider the new information in the environmental review.
IMPACT ON LAND
The 1.373-acre relatively flat project site is on Cherry Street and adjacent to Cayuga Inlet and the new
mixed-use development Ithaca Arthaus. Cherry Street is primarily characterized by industrial and
commercial land uses, while the Cayuga Inlet and its banks are used for passive recreation and boating.
The site is an industrial site that is currently was previously utilized by Weitsman Steel Service Center for
offices, a storage yard, and for parking and storage. Project implementation will required the demolition
and complete removal of the existing single-story masonry and pre-engineered metal building which is
approximately 11,500 sf and most of the vegetation within the limits of disturbance as indicated on the
Demolition Plan C2 dated April 28, 2020 and submitted by the applicants.
1

02/24/26 Amendment to the Negative Declaration issued on 8/23/22
City of Ithaca
FULL ENVIRONMENTAL ASSESSMENT FORM – Part III
Project Name: The Hive - 132 Cherry Street
Date Created: 05/03/22, Updated 5/10/22, 06/09/22, 06/20/22, 07/18/22, 08/04/22,
08/11/22, 08/16/22
Any text differing from the 8/23/22 FEAF is underlined
Construction is expected to last approximately 20 18 months and will occur in two phases from
approximately September 2022 through the beginning of 2024.
According to the FEAF Part 1, the applicant estimates approximately 2,000 CY of material will be excavated
from the project site.
The estimated depth to bedrock on the site ranges from approximately 68 feet to 100 feet below grade
as indicated in the Subsurface Investigation Report prepared by Elwyn & Palmer in February 2020. As a
result, no blasting is anticipated for construction. Building foundations are proposed deep foundation
system. The Report recommends use of a driven pile system to support the new building. The applicants
indicate in their memo dated 7/13/22, they will use “Controlled-Modulus-Column Rigid Inclusions
(CMCs) with a load mat to support typical spread footings bearing at frost depth” to support building
foundations and a “standard slab on grade concrete or asphalt construction with adequate stone
subbase and geotextile fabric for load distribution and support” for parking areas, drive lanes and
exterior parking areas.
The Lead Agency has determined that based on the information above, no significant impact to land is
anticipated.
IMPACT ON SURFACE WATER
The project site is adjacent to the Cayuga Inlet. Construction is anticipated to last approximately 20 18
months. During construction according to the E & S C9 Plan with a revision date of March 14, 2022,
submitted by the applicant, a silt fence will be located along the perimeter of the area of disturbance of
the project site to stop soil sediment and protect the Cayuga Inlet.
As total area of disturbance will be greater than 1 acre, the project team will submit a Basic SWPPP,
including erosion and sediment control practices during construction to the City of Ithaca in order to
comply with NYSDEC regulations.
The applicants are constructing a bio-retention swale along the western edge of the north building that
will be reviewed by the Lead Agency during Site Plan Review.
Therefore, based on the information above and complete adherence to an accepted SWPPP, the Lead
Agency has determined that no significant impact to surface water is anticipated.
IMPACT ON GROUNDWATER
Elwyn & Palmer performed a subsurface investigation of the project site in February 2020 which measured
groundwater depths 3.0 to 6.5 feet. According to the Subsurface Investigation Report, “Standing
2

02/24/26 Amendment to the Negative Declaration issued on 8/23/22
City of Ithaca
FULL ENVIRONMENTAL ASSESSMENT FORM – Part III
Project Name: The Hive - 132 Cherry Street
Date Created: 05/03/22, Updated 5/10/22, 06/09/22, 06/20/22, 07/18/22, 08/04/22,

groundwater was encountered in three of the borings at depths that would affect the construction.
Contractors should plan on incorporating dewatering efforts into their costs. Also, water infiltration into
the excavations during periods of wet weather may be problematic during construction and will need to
be directed away from and out of the excavation.” They recommend dewatering operations to route
groundwater and surface water away from the site and out of excavation.
The Lead Agency has determined that based on the information above and adherence to the
recommendations in the Subsurface Investigation Report, no significant impact to groundwater is
anticipated.
IMPACT ON FLOODING
There will be no net gain of impervious surface for the project. To mitigate for the proposed impervious
surfaces, the applicants are proposing a bioretention area along the western edge of the north building
and a robust planting plan.
The project site is located in a 100-year 500-year flood zone. The building must be raised 2’ above the BFE
and the portions of the building that are not elevated above the BFE must be wet or dry flood proofed.
The residential lobbies of both buildings are raised and therefore have ADA lifts to accommodate
accessibility.
The Lead Agency has determined that based on the information above, no significant impact on flooding
is anticipated.
IMPACTS ON AIR
According to information provided by the applicant, construction is projected to last approximately 20
months. During construction, generators may be required to provide power to the site. Excavation and
preparation of foundations additionally create the potential for increased airborne dust and dirt particles.
Impacts to air quality will be limited to the period associated with construction activities.
During construction, the applicant will employ the following applicable dust control measures, as
appropriate:
• Misting or fog spraying the site to minimize dust;
• Maintaining crushed stone tracking pads at all entrances to the construction site;
• Re-seeding disturbed areas to minimize bare exposed soils;
• Keeping roads clear of dust and debris;
• Requiring construction trucks to be covered; and
• Prohibiting burning of debris on site.
3

02/24/26 Amendment to the Negative Declaration issued on 8/23/22
City of Ithaca
FULL ENVIRONMENTAL ASSESSMENT FORM – Part III
Project Name: The Hive - 132 Cherry Street
Date Created: 05/03/22, Updated 5/10/22, 06/09/22, 06/20/22, 07/18/22, 08/04/22,
08/11/22, 08/16/22
Any text differing from the 8/23/22 FEAF is underlined
The Lead Agency has determined that with the mitigation measures during construction identified above,
no significant impact to air is anticipated.
IMPACT ON PLANTS AND ANIMALS
According to the Demolition Plan C2 dated April 28, 2020 and a memo dated 7/13/22, submitted by the
applicants the proposed redevelopment will require the removal of approximately 22 trees, which
primarily consist of spruce and apples (also one birch and two maples). Most of the larger trees are located
on the western side of the project site along the Cayuga Inlet and along Cherry Street. All the trees
identified for removal are 6” or greater DBH.
The Landscape Plan L-100 C-007 with a revision date of 7/18/22 2/10/26 and submitted by the applicants
proposes 108 large deciduous trees, 5 along Cherry Street, 7 large trees along the western property line,
and one large deciduous tree in the internal courtyard between the buildings. Several cherry maple trees,
many shrubs, and a fairly diverse palette of annuals and perennials are included in the landscape plan.
Wildlife likely to be encountered on or near the project site include invertebrates and occasional birds.
The New York Natural Heritage Program identifies the gray petaltail dragonfly as a species of “special
concern,” indicating that it is at risk of becoming threatened. The general habitat of the gray petaltail can
be described as hillside seeps and fens in areas of deciduous forest (Dunkle 2000). According to the New
York Natural Heritage Program:
“In New York, all known populations are found at rocky gorges and glens with deciduous
or mixed forests. Small shallow streams flow through the gorges and glens, and these
streams are fed by hillside seepage areas, groundwater fed seepage streamlets or fens.
The seepage areas represent the larval habitat for these populations, while the adults use
both the seepage areas and the stream courses.”
Population loss of this species has primarily been attributed to suburban development trends. The project
site is a previously developed site that is noted for relatively flat terrain, despite being located adjacent
to the Cayuga Inlet. In addition, no development is proposed on or near the banks, which is part of a
NYSDEC easement.
Based on the development history of the site and its historic uses, it is unlikely that any wildlife habitat
exists on the project site.
4

Based on the information provided above, the Lead Agency the Lead Agency has determined no significant
impact to plants and animals is anticipated.
IMPACT ON AGRICULTURAL RESOURCES
The project site is not in or adjacent to an agricultural area.
Based on the information provided above, the Lead Agency has determined no significant impact to
agricultural resources is anticipated.
IMPACT ON AESTHETIC RESOURCES
According to the Tompkins County Scenic Resource Views, there are no scenic resources located adjacent
to or in vicinity of the Project Site. Additionally, there are no locally identified scenic resources located
near the project site.
However, the project site will be highly visible from residential areas across the Inlet, waterfront natural
areas, open spaces and parks, and other vantage points within the waterway and around its edges. The
Lead Agency conducted Design Review at their June 28, 2022 meeting. They reviewed the Design Review
application dated 06/16/22 as well as drawings, renderings, visualizations, basis of design building
materials, and sections submitted dated June 2022.
The Planning Board/Lead Agency had the following feedback regarding design review at the 6/28/22
meeting:
• Would like to see depictions of the interface between the pool private space and the public space
along the inlet. There was concern from public that it could look defensive so would like it to look
appealing from inlet side. The applicant presented illustrations of the interior courtyard and pool
area at the July 26, 2022 Planning Board meeting.
• Think about downlighting for the commissioned murals
• They would like to see a more robust landscape along the western and southern sides of the
project. The applicants provided a more robust planting plan, Landscape Plan L100 dated
07/18/22 along the western side and will work with the city for the southern side of the building.
• There is some concern regarding the materiality of the buildings particularly the fiber cement and
the CMUs. General consensus that the colors are nice and the exterior design of the buildings
themselves, but would like to see better materials such as wood paneling or detailing The
applicants have provided material examples for the Lead Agency
• They think the unit to parking ratio is low and can accept the placement of the parking on the
ground floor although they would like to see more renderings of the screening both in materiality
around the space and a more robust landscaping The applicant presented renderings of the
screening at the July 26, 2022 Planning Board meeting.

illustrations of the interior courtyard and other spaces at the July 26, 2022 Planning Board
meeting.
Based on the information above, the Lead Agency has determined that no significant impacts to aesthetic
resources is anticipated.
IMPACT ON HISTORIC AND ARCHAEOLOGICAL RESOURCES
The site is not located within a historic district, and the existing site is not designated at the local or state
level as an historic resource.
According to the NYSDEC EAF Mapper, the site is located in an archaeologically sensitive area. However,
the site has experienced extensive prior disturbance based on its use for industrial/commercial purposes,
so no archaeological resources are anticipated to be encountered or disturbed during construction.
As a result of historic site use and based on the information provided above, the Lead Agency has
determined no significant impact on historic and archaeological resources is anticipated.
IMPACT ON OPEN SPACE AND RECREATION
The project site will be highly visible by users of the Inlet and the existing Cayuga Waterfront Trail, and to
proposed segments of the existing Black Diamond Trail. The Cayuga Waterfront Trail is a 5.5-mile multi-
use trail that connects many of Ithaca’s waterfront destinations, including Cass Park, the Farmers’ Market,
and the Visitors’ Center. The Black Diamond Trail is a 15-mile pedestrian and cyclist trail located on the
western shore of Cayuga Lake. The trail connects four major state parks, as well as other regional
community destinations. Improvements include development of segments from which the project site
may be visible. A bridge to connect bikers on the east side of the Cayuga Inlet to the Black Diamond Trail
is to be built on the southern side of the proposed building.
Both of these recreational resources, as well as Cass Park, currently have views of the Cherry Street
industrial area and the project site. As these corridors transition, efforts are being made to fit into the
community character while also accommodating changing land uses. The project aims to incorporate
architectural styles that are compatible with the surrounding area, while accommodating a transitioning
land use that is in synergy to the vision for the Enterprise Zone, as defined in Plan Ithaca. As such, no major
impact to open space and recreation is anticipated, but the project aims to improve the character of the
site from industrial to mixed-use with retail spaces and public access areas fronting the public trail to the
south of the project site, providing more public spaces and amenities. See also Impacts on Community
Character.
6

and recreation is anticipated.
IMPACT ON CRITICAL ENVIRONMENTAL AREAS
There are no critical environmental areas located within the City of Ithaca. However, Tompkins County
identifies Unique Natural Areas (“UNAs”) throughout the county, which are part of the landscape that has
outstanding geological and environmental qualities, such as special natural communities, or plants and
animals that are rare or scarce elsewhere in the county or region. A UNA is not a regulatory designation
and does not provide legal protection for an area but signals that special resources may exist that require
project modification.
The closest UNA to the Project Site is Octopus Cliffs (UNA 137), which is located across the Cayuga Inlet
from the site. Although the project will be visible, the applicant is carefully considering architectural
materials and colors that will coincide with the surrounding transitional industrial corridor, and be less
impactful to natural areas, residences, and parks that may be impacted by the structure.
As a result of the information provided above and in discussions with the applicant, the Lead Agency has
determined no significant impact to Critical Environmental Areas is anticipated.
IMPACT ON TRANSPORTATION
The proposed project is primarily a residential development with private amenities, three commercial
spaces, and potential community art spaces located on the ground floors of the buildings.
The site will accommodate on-site parking spaces for residential parking and seven parallel parking spaces
along Cherry Street. The ground floors will house approximately +/- 43 40 covered parking spaces, and 8
6 additional spaces will be available along Cherry Street.
The project team is in discussions with the city regarding creating a streetscape design in front of their
building that will fit within a larger streetscape plan for the entirety of Cherry Street. This plan includes
the ground floor to be in-line with the adjacent Arthaus façade, a double bike lane, on-street parking, 5-
6’ wide tree lawns, 6’ wide sidewalks, and a 8’ tree lawn which provides 3 pockets of 2 parrallel parking
spaces each, along with 10-12’ drive lanes, and TCAT bus service. The applicants are also in discussion
with TCAT regarding the potential location of bus stops and services on or near the project site. According
to a memo from the applicants dated 08/16/22 and the Ground Floor/Site Plan A100 dated 07/29/22
submitted by the applicant, there are 74 total proposed bike spaces. The south building will have 40
interior bike spaces, 10 in the parking garage, and 6 bike racks which will hold 12 bikes on the exterior
while the north building will have 6 outdoor bike racks with 12 spaces for bikes.

As a result of the information provided above, the Lead Agency has determined that no significant impact

IMPACT ON ENERGY
On August 4, 2021, the Ithaca Energy Code Supplement (IECS) went into effect for all new buildings
constructed in Ithaca. The IECS prioritizes electrification, renewable energy, and affordability with the
following objectives:
“deliver measurable and immediate reductions in greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions from new
buildings, major renovations, and new additions; promote best practices in the design of
affordable buildings to deliver reduced GHG emissions; and provide a rapid but orderly
transition to buildings that do not use fossil fuels for major building energy needs such as space
heating and hot water heating, by 2026. For construction subject to the Ithaca Energy Code
Supplement, requirements for reductions in GHGs go into effect in three steps: 2021, 2023,
and 2026.”
From August 4, 2021, until 2023 all new buildings must produce 40% fewer greenhouse gas emissions
than the Energy Conservation Construction Code of New York State requires. Beginning in 2023, the IECS
will increase the requirements of new construction to produce 80% fewer greenhouse gas emissions than
the Energy Conservation Construction Code of New York State requires, and by 2026 all newly constructed
buildings in Ithaca will be required to be net-zero buildings that do not use fossil fuels. The IECS supports
Ithaca’s Green New Deal which aims to “achieve an equitable transition to carbon-neutrality” community-
wide by 2030.
The Building Division will oversee implementation and enforcement of the IECS.
As a result, from the information provided above, the Lead Agency has determined that no significant
impact to energy is anticipated.
IMPACT ON NOISE, ODOR, AND LIGHT
The project will produce temporary construction noise, particularly during pile driving. According to the
site plan application provided by the applicant, construction is anticipated to last approximately 20
months. The project is located in an industrial area and some commercial activity and is adjacent to a new
mixed-use residential building.
The building foundation will use Controlled Modulus Rigid Inclusions (CMCs) with a load mat to support
typical spread footings at frost depth. The estimated depth to bedrock on the site ranges from
approximately 68 feet to 100 feet below grade. As a result, no blasting is anticipated for construction.
This has the potential to produce temporary noise impacts in the area and across the inlet.

IMPACT ON HUMAN HEALTH
The project site has no reported spills in the NYDEC Spills Incidents database or in the Environmental
Remediation database. The project site is located within 2000’ of 110 Cherry Street, NYSDEC
Environmental Site Remediation Site ID, C755020. 110 Cherry Street is a Brownfield Cleanup Program Site
for which a Remedial Investigation Work Plan was created in September 2021 by C&S Engineers, Inc. 110
Cherry Street will be remediated through the program.
The project site is also adjacent to 130 Cherry Street which was a former automotive repair facility and
filling station that operated on the site since the 1970s until recently when it was redeveloped into a multi-
use residential building, Ithaca Arthaus. Before development, a Phase I Environmental Site Assessment
(“ESA”) was completed for 130 Cherry St and this along with the results of the Focused Investigation. As is
described in the Focused Investigation Report and in the Environmental Risk Management Summary
(“ERMS”), “no contamination was observed in soil at the Site above regulatory levels of the State of New
York.”
The existing building will be completely removed and replaced. Demolition may include removal of toxic
substances such as asbestos or lead paint. Removal and disposal of any hazardous building materials will
be done by a licensed contractor and in accordance with State and local Laws
As a result of this information, the Lead Agency has determined no significant impact to human
health is anticipated.
CONSISTENCY WITH COMMUNITY PLANS
The project, as proposed, is consistent with the future land use plans expressed in the City of Ithaca’s 2015
Comprehensive Plan (“Plan Ithaca”). Plan Ithaca recognizes the area as the City’s industrial park,
established in the 1990s. The plan identifies the Cherry Street corridor as an Enterprise future land use
area, defined as areas targeted for expansion of business and employment opportunities as well as
particular residential uses.

The Hive: Project Narrative
Overview
The Hive at 132 Cherry Street is a previously approved five-story, mixed-use development
located in the Cherry Street District (CSD). The project received site plan approval in 2022 and
was originally designed as market-rate housing. In response to Ithaca’s housing needs, Visum
Development has since transitioned the project to affordable housing and intends to pursue
HCR funding. The project’s existing site plan approval remains valid through an approved
extension; however, the development team is now seeking an amended site plan approval due
to FEMA floodplain map revisions that require modifications to the approved design. The project
will also require setback variances from the BZA and amended SEQR recommendations.
The development team has made a concerted effort to maintain the market-rate quality of the
project while providing more affordable housing options for the city of Ithaca. Minimal changes
have been implemented during this redesign only to address FEMA regulations and HCR
requirements.
ARCHITECTURE BUILDING COMMUNITY Page 1of 4
WWW.CJSARCHITECTS.COM

Recommendations made by the Board during February 13th PRC Meeting
What will make this a successful project? How does architecture, design and programming
address equitability and accessibility between the two buildings?
This project provides affordable and workforce housing for its residents; creating
population density into the neighborhood will help stabilize the community. Arthaus is
currently an island in the CSD, by adding more diversity it will help create a more
equitable and prosperous society.
Splitting the massing into two buildings instead of one provides the density required to
building a feasible project while maintaining openness without the building appearing
large and oppressive along the street. The façade breaks up the massing with
architectural pushes and pulls at the upper levels and to highlight points of entries at the
ground level. The ground floor wraps its programming in a way that best activates the
two main frontages of the property. The FEMA setback created a unique opportunity to
interact with the black diamond trail by extending a towpath along the property line
that would be a public amenity as well. The developer and management are in favor of
providing amenities such as a gym along the trail that would be accessible to both the
residents and the greater community via membership.
The two buildings provide unique amenities and vantage points for the residents while still
maintaining shared spaces to foster interaction between the tenants and the city
residents. The North building, although it does not host the resident amenity spaces,
provides the most units with waterfront views. The residents are encouraged to interact
with the community by accessing the other buildings and amenities via the sidewalk
along Cherry St further activating the street and creating more vibrancy to the
neighborhood. Management experience shares that different people prioritize different
needs, some require amenities within walking distance while others prefer them in the
same building, some prefer water views while others prefer city views. This project
provides a variety of options for the residents so that they can choose which unit would
best serve their individual needs.
From a management perspective clustering the amenities together provides better
control, safety and maintenance of the spaces. We believe that the idea of bridging the
two building at upper levels negates the desire to create a cohesive community and
activate Cherry St. Management is willing and able to provide the necessary
maintenance to sidewalks to create a year-round accessible route from one building to
the other along Cherry St. The management also raises safety concerns around mixing
private circulation in the corridors with more public circulation to create a through-
passage for residents so they can have interior access to both buildings.
Stormwater Plan:
The project will maintain the previously approved stormwater plan and will not be
connected to the city system. Please refer to the Landscape Plan L-100 and E&S Plan in
the civil package for more information.

February 18, 2026
Re: The Hive/132 Cherry St Zoning Variance Request Narrative
To Whom it May Concern,
This narrative is to inform you of the proposed Hive project at 132 Cherry St and a proposed zoning
variance request that will be pursued for this development. Visum Development is proposing five-
story, mixed-use, affordable housing project called The Hive at 132 Cherry Street, located in the
Cherry Street District (CSD). The two buildings will include 148 residential units total including 12
studios, 88 one-bedrooms, and 48 two-bedrooms. The building’s amenities include a fitness room,
community room, indoor bicycle storage, dog wash, and secure package rooms. The ground floor
of the buildings will include +/- 40 covered parking spaces. There are two ground-floor commercial
spaces on Cherry St; the northern space will comprise approx. 1,400 square feet and the southern
space approx. 1,400 square feet. There is also an approx.1,400 sf commercial space on the SW
corner adjacent to the Waterfront Trail and pedestrian bridge. There is a plaza located along
Cherry Street and a waterfront courtyard with outdoor recreation space.
The total square footage of the building is approx. 175,000 sf, and the building footprint is 33,500
sf. A pergola adds an additional 2000 sf +/-. The lot is 1.37 acres, resulting in 56% proposed lot
coverage. Over 20% of the site is green space that is either grass or landscaped.
The majority of the building’s height is 58’, portions of the roof reach 63’. There is a single curb
cut, situated between the two buildings to allow ground level parking access to the proposed
buildings and entry plaza. A tree lawn integrated with parallel parking spaces and sidewalk have
been integrated into the design on the property.
The project is seeking an Area Variance for the Section 325-8 Required Minimum Front Yard in the
Cherry Street District (CSD) - 15’ minimum required front yard setback measured from the inside
edge of the sidewalk. (Amended 2020). Previously this project requested and obtained an area
variance for the Front Yard requirement. Since that time, the newly adopted FEMA maps put the
entire site in the 1% flood area and the Regulatory Floodway encroaches up to 25’ on the rear of
the property. The Regulatory floodway is a no build area. In order to maintain the number of units
for a viable project we have to move the buildings east out of the Regulatory Floodway. We have
also shortened the north leg of the northern building and relocated some units to the southern leg.
We propose moving the ground floor of the buildings in line with the adjacent Arthaus building to
the north (15’ from the property line); the ground floor of the southern building would also be 15’
from the property line with some portion (approx. 50%) of the floor above extending out another
5’ to 10’ from the property line. This overhang would be approximately 11’ 0” above grade, We
are proposing to place the outside edge of the sidewalk 6” inside the property line with an 8’ tree
lawn integrated with parallel parking spaces in the R.O.W. between the sidewalk and road curb.
We propose a 6’ wide sidewalk with the inner edge 8 ’6” from the face of building at the ground
floor. Some portions of the upper floors would extend and additional 5’0” as described above.

WWW.CJSARCHITECTS.COM

We request a setback of 15’0” minimum @ the first floor and 10’0” minimum at portions of the floors
above to be measured from the property line. This would put the face of the buildings at 18’ -23’
maximum from the edge of road, meeting the aerial access requirements of the Fire Code of NYS
which was the basis of our previous variance request. The buildings have a combined frontage of
approximately 260’. Approximately 90 linear feet of the upper floors of south building would
overhang the first floor as described above. The ground floor and majority of the upper floors
would be in line with the adjacent building. This provides a more interesting and desirable
streetscape by breaking up the building mass into smaller pieces.
We have worked in concert with city planning officials to meet the desire for a tree lawn
integrated with parallel parking spaces between both the street and sidewalk and a tree lawn
between the sidewalk and building, we feel that the proposed balances those desires with the
need to relocate the buildings out of the Regulatory Floodway on the newly adopted FEMA maps
and provide a more desirable vantage point for the pedestrian as they walk along a tree-lined
Cherry St.

accessible to both the residents and the greater community.

Any text differing from the 8/23/22 FEAF is underlined
PROJECT DESCRIPTION
The applicant proposes to demolish the existing building and construct a primarily residential mixed-use
affordable housing development. The applicant proposes two 5-story buildings, with a combined total
gross floor area of 160,800 SF 174,622 SF. The buildings contain 148 143 residential units on four floors,
three commercial spaces totaling approximately 4,200 4,350 SF, 40 43 +/- parking spaces on the ground
floor, and indoor amenities including a fitness room, multifunctional studio, community room kitchen ,
rooftop terrace and lounge, dog wash, indoor bicycle storage, and secured package rooms. Outdoor
amenities include a picnic area, a waterfront courtyard with a pool, a plaza along Cherry Street,
streetscape improvements and landscaping. The project is located in in the Cherry Street District Zoning
District and will require a front yard several variances. The project site is in the Cherry Street Zoning
District (CSD) and is subject to Design Review.
This has been determined to be a Type 1 Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental Quality Review
Ordinance §176-4 B(1) (h)[2], (k), and (n) and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”)
§617.4 (b)(11) and is subject to environmental review for which Planning Board, acting as Lead Agency,
made a negative declaration of environmental significance on August 23, 2022.
After filing the Negative Declaration, the applicant was required to alter the design to address the newly
issued FEMA flood maps as the building is now located in the 100-year floodplain, Zone AE flood hazard
area. The design was also altered to meet New York State Homes and Community Renewal (HCR)
requirements as the applicants are pursuing HCR funding for affordable housing. Changes in design
include raising the residential lobbies by 42” so that they are 2’ above Base Flood Elevation (BFE),
installing ADU lifts in the lobbies, moving the buildings east towards Cherry Street, and reconfiguring the
building unit footprints and unit configuration.
In accordance with §176-7 E. of CEQR and §617.7(e) of SEQRA, the Lead Agency decided to amend this
negative determination in order to consider the new information in the environmental review.
IMPACT ON LAND
The 1.373-acre relatively flat project site is on Cherry Street and adjacent to Cayuga Inlet and the new
mixed-use development Ithaca Arthaus. Cherry Street is primarily characterized by industrial and
commercial land uses, while the Cayuga Inlet and its banks are used for passive recreation and boating.
The site is an industrial site that is currently was previously utilized by Weitsman Steel Service Center for
offices, a storage yard, and for parking and storage. Project implementation will required the demolition
and complete removal of the existing single-story masonry and pre-engineered metal building which is
approximately 11,500 sf and most of the vegetation within the limits of disturbance as indicated on the
Demolition Plan C2 dated April 28, 2020 and submitted by the applicants.

Construction is expected to last approximately 20 18 months and will occur in two phases from
approximately September 2022 through the beginning of 2024.
According to the FEAF Part 1, the applicant estimates approximately 2,000 CY of material will be excavated
from the project site.
The estimated depth to bedrock on the site ranges from approximately 68 feet to 100 feet below grade
as indicated in the Subsurface Investigation Report prepared by Elwyn & Palmer in February 2020. As a
result, no blasting is anticipated for construction. Building foundations are proposed deep foundation
system. The Report recommends use of a driven pile system to support the new building. The applicants
indicate in their memo dated 7/13/22, they will use “Controlled-Modulus-Column Rigid Inclusions
(CMCs) with a load mat to support typical spread footings bearing at frost depth” to support building
foundations and a “standard slab on grade concrete or asphalt construction with adequate stone
subbase and geotextile fabric for load distribution and support” for parking areas, drive lanes and
exterior parking areas.
The Lead Agency has determined that based on the information above, no significant impact to land is
anticipated.
IMPACT ON SURFACE WATER
The project site is adjacent to the Cayuga Inlet. Construction is anticipated to last approximately 20 18
months. During construction according to the E & S C9 Plan with a revision date of March 14, 2022,
submitted by the applicant, a silt fence will be located along the perimeter of the area of disturbance of
the project site to stop soil sediment and protect the Cayuga Inlet.
As total area of disturbance will be greater than 1 acre, the project team will submit a Basic SWPPP,
including erosion and sediment control practices during construction to the City of Ithaca in order to
comply with NYSDEC regulations.
The applicants are constructing a bio-retention swale along the western edge of the north building that
will be reviewed by the Lead Agency during Site Plan Review.
Therefore, based on the information above and complete adherence to an accepted SWPPP, the Lead
Agency has determined that no significant impact to surface water is anticipated.
IMPACT ON GROUNDWATER
Elwyn & Palmer performed a subsurface investigation of the project site in February 2020 which measured
groundwater depths 3.0 to 6.5 feet. According to the Subsurface Investigation Report, “Standing

IMPACT ON FLOODING
There will be no net gain of impervious surface for the project. To mitigate for the proposed impervious
surfaces, the applicants are proposing a bioretention area along the western edge of the north building
and a robust planting plan.
The project site is located in a 100-year 500-year flood zone. The building must be raised 2’ above the BFE
and the portions of the building that are not elevated above the BFE must be wet or dry flood proofed.
The Lead Agency has determined that based on the information above, no significant impact on flooding
is anticipated.
IMPACTS ON AIR
According to information provided by the applicant, construction is projected to last approximately 20
months. During construction, generators may be required to provide power to the site. Excavation and
preparation of foundations additionally create the potential for increased airborne dust and dirt particles.
Impacts to air quality will be limited to the period associated with construction activities.
During construction, the applicant will employ the following applicable dust control measures, as
appropriate:
(cid:120) Misting or fog spraying the site to minimize dust;
(cid:120) Maintaining crushed stone tracking pads at all entrances to the construction site;
(cid:120) Re-seeding disturbed areas to minimize bare exposed soils;
(cid:120) Keeping roads clear of dust and debris;
(cid:120) Requiring construction trucks to be covered; and
(cid:120) Prohibiting burning of debris on site.
The Lead Agency has determined that with the mitigation measures during construction identified above,
no significant impact to air is anticipated.

IMPACT ON PLANTS AND ANIMALS
According to the Demolition Plan C2 dated April 28, 2020 and a memo dated 7/13/22, submitted by the
applicants the proposed redevelopment will require the removal of approximately 22 trees, which
primarily consist of spruce and apples (also one birch and two maples). Most of the larger trees are located
on the western side of the project site along the Cayuga Inlet and along Cherry Street. All the trees
identified for removal are 6” or greater DBH.
The Landscape Plan L-100 with a revision date of 7/18/22 and submitted by the applicants proposes 10
large deciduous trees along Cherry Street, 7 large trees along the western property line, and one large
deciduous tree in the internal courtyard between the buildings. Several cherry trees, many shrubs, and a
fairly diverse palette of annuals and perennials are included in the landscape plan.
Wildlife likely to be encountered on or near the project site include invertebrates and occasional birds.
The New York Natural Heritage Program identifies the gray petaltail dragonfly as a species of “special
concern,” indicating that it is at risk of becoming threatened. The general habitat of the gray petaltail can
be described as hillside seeps and fens in areas of deciduous forest (Dunkle 2000). According to the New
York Natural Heritage Program:
“In New York, all known populations are found at rocky gorges and glens with deciduous
or mixed forests. Small shallow streams flow through the gorges and glens, and these
streams are fed by hillside seepage areas, groundwater fed seepage streamlets or fens.
The seepage areas represent the larval habitat for these populations, while the adults use
both the seepage areas and the stream courses.”
Population loss of this species has primarily been attributed to suburban development trends. The project
site is a previously developed site that is noted for relatively flat terrain, despite being located adjacent
to the Cayuga Inlet. In addition, no development is proposed on or near the banks, which is part of a
NYSDEC easement.
Based on the development history of the site and its historic uses, it is unlikely that any wildlife habitat
exists on the project site.
Based on the information provided above, the Lead Agency the Lead Agency has determined no significant
impact to plants and animals is anticipated.

Based on the information above, the Lead Agency has determined that no significant impacts to aesthetic
